     1→# Stabilize System Prompt for Local LLM KV Cache
     2→
     3→## Problem
     4→
     5→Local LLM backends (Ollama, LM Studio) use KV cache to skip re-processing
     6→tokens that are identical to the previous request's prefix. Moltis invalidates
     7→this cache on **every turn** because the system prompt contains a per-second
     8→datetime string that changes each request. This forces 15-20s of full prompt
     9→re-ingestion on every message, even though the actual conversation content is
    10→mostly unchanged.
    11→
    12→When the same question is asked directly in LM Studio (no system prompt, no
    13→tools), the response is instant because the KV cache prefix matches.
    14→
    15→## Root Causes
    16→
    17→### 1. Per-second datetime in system prompt (primary)
    18→
    19→`crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1282` — `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` is called per
    20→turn, setting `host.time` to the current second. This flows into two places in
    21→the rendered system prompt:
    22→
    23→- **Runtime section** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs:497`): `Host: ... time=2026-03-23 14:32:17 CET`
    24→- **Tail line** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs:726`): `The current user datetime is 2026-03-23 14:32:17 CET.`
    25→
    26→Since the system prompt is the **first** content in the rendered prompt, any
    27→change invalidates the KV cache for **all** subsequent tokens (entire
    28→conversation history + tool schemas).
    29→
    30→### 2. Dynamic node telemetry in Runtime section
    31→
    32→When remote nodes are connected, `cpu_usage`, `mem_available`, `disk_available`
    33→are included in the `## Runtime` section. These change frequently and also
    34→invalidate the cache.
    35→
    36→### 3. Tool schemas are large but stable (amplifier, not cause)
    37→
    38→Tool schemas add ~1000-3000 tokens per request. They don't change between
    39→turns, but they must be fully re-ingested whenever the system prompt prefix
    40→changes. This amplifies the cost of cause #1.
    41→
    42→## Plan
    43→
    44→### Phase 1: Stabilize datetime within a session (quick win, biggest impact)
    45→
    46→**Goal**: Make the system prompt byte-identical between turns within the same
    47→session so the KV cache prefix matches.
    48→
    49→#### Option A: Coarsen datetime to date-only for local providers (recommended)
    50→
    51→In `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1282`), when the
    52→provider is a local backend (Ollama, LM Studio, or any provider with a
    53→localhost/private base URL), set `host.time = None` and only set `host.today`
    54→(date without time). The date changes once per day, so the KV cache is valid
    55→for an entire day's session.
    56→
    57→**Files to change:**
    58→
    59→1. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — Add a `is_local_provider` check (or pass a
    60→   flag) to `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()`. When local, skip setting
    61→   `host.time` and only set `host.today`.
    62→
    63→2. **`crates/providers/src/lib.rs`** — Expose a way to determine if a provider
    64→   is local. The Ollama registration path already knows this (`def.config_name
    65→   == "ollama"`). Consider adding a `is_local: bool` field to `ModelInfo` or a
    66→   method on the provider trait.
    67→
    68→3. **`crates/agents/src/model.rs`** — Add `fn is_local(&self) -> bool` to
    69→   `LlmProvider` trait with a default `false` implementation. Override in
    70→   `OpenAiProvider` when the base URL is localhost/private.
    71→
    72→**Alternatively** (simpler, less targeted): Cache the runtime context per
    73→session and only regenerate it every N minutes or when config changes. This
    74→avoids the local-vs-remote distinction but means cloud providers also get a
    75→slightly stale datetime.
    76→
    77→#### Option B: Pin datetime at session start
    78→
    79→Store the datetime when the session starts and reuse it for all subsequent
    80→turns. This is the simplest approach but means the LLM sees a stale time if the
    81→conversation runs for hours.
    82→
    83→**Files to change:**
    84→
    85→1. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — In `build_prompt_runtime_context()`, accept
    86→   an optional `pinned_time: Option<String>`. If provided, use it instead of
    87→   calling `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()`.
    88→
    89→2. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — In the main chat handler (around line 3457),
    90→   store the first runtime context's time value and pass it to subsequent
    91→   calls.
    92→
    93→### Phase 2: Stabilize node telemetry (if nodes are used)
    94→
    95→When remote nodes are connected, their telemetry (`cpu_usage`, `mem_available`)
    96→changes between turns. Two options:
    97→
    98→1. **Exclude volatile telemetry from the system prompt** — Move `cpu_usage`,
    99→   `mem_available`, `disk_available` out of the Runtime section. The LLM rarely
   100→   needs real-time resource stats in the system prompt.
   101→
   102→2. **Round/bucket telemetry values** — Round CPU to nearest 10%, memory to
   103→   nearest GB. This reduces churn while keeping the info available.
   104→
   105→**Files to change:**
   106→
   107→- `crates/agents/src/prompt.rs` — `format_nodes_runtime_section()` (around line
   108→  508). Either skip volatile fields or round them.
   109→
   110→### Phase 3: Move datetime to end of messages (structural improvement)
   111→
   112→Even with Phase 1, the datetime is inside the system prompt which is the first
   113→message. An alternative architecture: remove datetime from the system prompt
   114→entirely and inject it as a short system message **at the end** of the message
   115→list, just before the latest user message. This way, even if the datetime
   116→changes, only the last few tokens need re-processing (the KV cache for the
   117→entire prior conversation is preserved).
   118→
   119→**Files to change:**
   120→
   121→1. **`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`** — Remove `append_runtime_datetime_tail()`
   122→   from `build_system_prompt_full()`.
   123→
   124→2. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — In `run_with_tools()` (around line 6040),
   125→   remove `apply_voice_reply_suffix` datetime handling. Instead, after building
   126→   the message history, append a `ChatMessage::System` with the datetime just
   127→   before sending to the provider.
   128→
   129→3. **`crates/providers/src/openai.rs`** — In `serialize_messages_for_request()`,
   130→   ensure trailing system messages are preserved (they currently are, since
   131→   system messages just map to `{"role": "system", ...}`).
   132→
   133→**Trade-off**: Some providers/models handle mid-conversation system messages
   134→poorly. Test with Ollama + Qwen specifically. This approach gives the best KV
   135→cache behavior but needs validation.
   136→
   137→### Phase 4: Explore Ollama-native API (future)
   138→
   139→Moltis currently uses Ollama's OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions`
   140→endpoint. Ollama's native `/api/chat` endpoint offers:
   141→
   142→- Explicit `keep_alive` parameter to control model unload timeout
   143→- Same KV caching behavior but with more control
   144→- Native format avoids OpenAI compatibility layer overhead
   145→
   146→This is a larger change and not required if Phases 1-3 resolve the issue. Park
   147→it unless users report additional problems.
   148→
   149→## Recommended Implementation Order
   150→
   151→1. **Phase 1 Option A** — Biggest bang for buck. Stabilize datetime for local
   152→   providers. This alone should eliminate the 15-20s re-ingestion.
   153→2. **Phase 2** — Only if users have remote nodes connected and still see cache
   154→   misses.
   155→3. **Phase 3** — Best long-term architecture but more invasive. Do this if
   156→   Phase 1 isn't sufficient or if you want to optimize for cloud providers too
   157→   (Anthropic prompt caching also benefits from stable prefixes).
   158→4. **Phase 4** — Future work, only if needed.
   159→
   160→## Validation
   161→
   162→- Set up Ollama with Qwen 3.4 locally
   163→- Send 3-4 messages in a conversation
   164→- Before fix: observe prompt eval time in Ollama logs (`prompt eval time: ...`)
   165→  growing with each turn and re-processing all tokens
   166→- After fix: observe prompt eval time staying small (only new tokens processed)
   167→- Ollama debug logs: `OLLAMA_DEBUG=1 ollama serve` shows cache hit/miss info
   168→- Also verify cloud providers (OpenAI, Anthropic) still get correct datetime
   169→
```

Now let me create a comprehensive summary document of my findings:

## Summary

I have thoroughly explored the datetime and runtime context code in this Rust project. Here's a detailed analysis of how the system prompt datetime gets populated and injected:

### Data Flow Architecture

**1. Runtime Context Structs** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`, lines 44-133):
- `PromptHostRuntimeContext` - Host execution context with `time` and `today` fields (lines 46-81)
  - `time: Option<String>` - Current datetime string (localized when timezone is known)
  - `today: Option<String>` - Current date string (YYYY-MM-DD format)
  - `timezone: Option<String>` - Timezone configuration
- `PromptRuntimeContext` - Combined context with host, sandbox, and nodes (lines 129-133)

**2. Context Building** (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`, lines 1165-1280):
- `build_prompt_runtime_context()` (lines 1165-1280) - Async function that constructs `PromptRuntimeContext`
  - Gets timezone from sandbox router config (line 1210-1213)
  - Creates `PromptHostRuntimeContext` with base fields (lines 1222-1243)
  - **Calls `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` at line 1244** to populate `time` and `today`

**3. Time Calculation** (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`, lines 1282-1350):
- `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` (lines 1282-1285) - Sets `time` and `today` fields:
  ```rust
  fn refresh_runtime_prompt_time(host: &mut PromptHostRuntimeContext) {
      host.time = Some(prompt_now_for_timezone(host.timezone.as_deref()));
      host.today = Some(prompt_today_for_timezone(host.timezone.as_deref()));
  }
  ```

- `prompt_now_for_timezone()` (lines 1297-1324) - Returns formatted datetime:
  - With `web-ui` or `push-notifications` features: Uses `chrono` crate to parse IANA timezone and format as `"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z"`
  - Fallback: Uses `Local::now()` for unknown/non-IANA timezones
  - CLI only: Returns unix timestamp format

- `prompt_today_for_timezone()` (lines 1326-1350) - Returns formatted date (YYYY-MM-DD):
  - Uses same timezone resolution logic as `prompt_now_for_timezone()`

**4. Request-Time Timezone Override** (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`, lines 1368-1386):
- `apply_request_runtime_context()` (lines 1368-1386) - Updates context from request parameters:
  - Extracts `_timezone` parameter from request
  - Falls back to user's configured timezone via `default_user_prompt_timezone()`
  - **Re-calls `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` at line 1385** to recompute time/today with new timezone

- `normalized_iana_timezone()` (lines 1352-1358) - Validates and normalizes timezone strings using `chrono_tz::Tz`

**5. Prompt Construction** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`, lines 354-392):
- `build_system_prompt_full()` (lines 354-392) - Assembles the complete system prompt:
  - Line 381: `append_runtime_section()` - Adds runtime host/sandbox/nodes context
  - Line 389: **`append_runtime_datetime_tail()` - Appends datetime sentence at the end**

**6. DateTime Injection into Prompt** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`, lines 712-742):
- `append_runtime_datetime_tail()` (lines 712-742) - Adds final datetime sentence:
  - If `runtime.host.time` is set: Appends `"The current user datetime is {time}.\n"`
  - Otherwise, if `runtime.host.today` is set: Appends `"The current user date is {today}.\n"`
  - Returns early if neither is available

**7. Runtime Line in Prompt** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`, lines 750-785):
- `format_host_runtime_line()` (lines 750-785) - Formats host runtime as single-line key=value pairs:
  - Includes `today` field (line 757) in the "Host: ..." line format
  - Does NOT include `time` field (only `today`)
  - Full host line format: `Host: host=... | os=... | today=... | timezone=... | ...`

### Key Integration Points

**Chat Handler** (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`, lines 3457-3464):
```rust
let mut runtime_context = build_prompt_runtime_context(
    &self.state,
    &provider,
    &session_key,
    session_entry.as_ref(),
).await;
apply_request_runtime_context(&mut runtime_context.host, &params);
```

**System Prompt Building** (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`, lines 6011-6041):
```rust
let system_prompt = build_system_prompt_with_session_runtime(
    &filtered_registry,
    native_tools,
    project_context,
    skills,
    Some(&persona.identity),
    Some(&persona.user),
    persona.soul_text.as_deref(),
    persona.agents_text.as_deref(),
    persona.tools_text.as_deref(),
    runtime_context,
    persona.memory_text.as_deref(),
);
let system_prompt = apply_voice_reply_suffix(system_prompt, desired_reply_medium, runtime_context);
```

**Agent Loop** (`crates/agents/src/runner.rs`, lines 1225-1256):
```rust

    provider: Arc<dyn LlmProvider>,
    tools: &ToolRegistry,
    system_prompt: &str,  // <-- contains datetime appended
    user_content: &UserContent,
    ...
) -> Result<AgentRunResult, AgentRunError> {
    let mut messages: Vec<ChatMessage> = vec![ChatMessage::system(system_prompt)];
    // ...
}
```

### LLM Provider Trait

`crates/agents/src/model.rs` (lines 354-449):
- `LlmProvider` trait has methods:
  - `fn complete()` - Single-shot completion
  - `fn stream()` - Streaming completion
  - `fn stream_with_tools()` - Streaming with tool support
  - No special datetime handling at provider level

### Test Coverage

`crates/chat/src/lib.rs` (lines 9230-9287):
- Line 9241: `apply_request_runtime_context_uses_request_timezone()` - Validates request timezone override
- Line 9262: `apply_voice_reply_suffix_keeps_datetime_tail_at_end()` - Ensures datetime stays at prompt end

### Feature Flags

The datetime formatting changes based on feature flags:
- **With `web-ui` or `push-notifications`**: Full timezone-aware datetime using `chrono` crate
- **Without**: Simplified unix timestamp format fallback

### Critical Datetime Flow Summary

1. `build_prompt_runtime_context()` creates base context with `time=None, today=None`
2. `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` computes time/today strings based on timezone
3. `apply_request_runtime_context()` updates timezone from request params and re-refreshes time
4. `build_system_prompt_full()` calls `append_runtime_datetime_tail()` to append sentence
5. System prompt is passed to `run_agent_loop_streaming()`
6. Chat message vector receives system message with datetime-populated prompt
7. Provider receives system message as first item in messages array

  1287→fn server_prompt_timezone(configured_timezone: Option<&str>) -> String {
  1288→    if let Some(timezone) = configured_timezone
  1289→        .map(str::trim)
  1290→        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())
  1291→    {
  1292→        return timezone.to_string();
  1293→    }
  1294→    "server-local".to_string()
  1295→}
  1296→
  1297→fn prompt_now_for_timezone(timezone: Option<&str>) -> String {
  1298→    #[cfg(any(feature = "web-ui", feature = "push-notifications"))]
  1299→    {
  1300→        use chrono::{Local, Utc};
  1301→
  1302→        let trimmed_timezone = timezone.map(str::trim).filter(|value| !value.is_empty());
  1303→
  1304→        if let Some(tz) = trimmed_timezone.and_then(|name| name.parse::<chrono_tz::Tz>().ok()) {
  1305→            return Utc::now()
  1306→                .with_timezone(&tz)
  1307→                .format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z")
  1308→                .to_string();
  1309→        }
  1310→
  1311→        // Fallback to server local clock when timezone is unknown/non-IANA.
  1312→        Local::now().format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z").to_string()
  1313→    }
  1314→
  1315→    #[cfg(not(any(feature = "web-ui", feature = "push-notifications")))]
  1316→    {
  1317→        let unix_secs = std::time::SystemTime::now()
  1318→            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
  1319→            .unwrap_or_default()
  1320→            .as_secs();
  1321→        let tz = timezone.unwrap_or("server-local");
  1322→        format!("unix={unix_secs} {tz}")
  1323→    }
  1324→}
  1325→
  1326→fn prompt_today_for_timezone(timezone: Option<&str>) -> String {
  1327→    #[cfg(any(feature = "web-ui", feature = "push-notifications"))]
  1328→    {
  1329→        use chrono::{Local, Utc};
  1330→
  1331→        let trimmed_timezone = timezone.map(str::trim).filter(|value| !value.is_empty());
  1332→
  1333→        if let Some(tz) = trimmed_timezone.and_then(|name| name.parse::<chrono_tz::Tz>().ok()) {
  1334→            return Utc::now().with_timezone(&tz).format("%Y-%m-%d").to_string();
  1335→        }
  1336→
  1337→        Local::now().format("%Y-%m-%d").to_string()
  1338→    }
  1339→
  1340→    #[cfg(not(any(feature = "web-ui", feature = "push-notifications")))]
  1341→    {
  1342→        let _ = timezone;
  1343→        let unix_days = std::time::SystemTime::now()

    83→/// Runtime context for sandbox execution routing used by the `exec` tool.

    97→    /// Per-session override for sandbox enablement.

    99→}
   100→
   101→/// Info about a single connected remote node, injected into the system prompt.
   102→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
   103→pub struct PromptNodeInfo {
   104→    pub node_id: String,
   105→    pub display_name: Option<String>,
   106→    pub platform: String,

   108→    pub cpu_count: Option<u32>,

   112→    pub telemetry_stale: bool,
   113→    pub disk_total: Option<u64>,
   114→    pub disk_available: Option<u64>,
   115→    pub runtimes: Vec<String>,
   116→    /// `(provider_name, model_list)` pairs discovered on the node.
   117→    pub providers: Vec<(String, Vec<String>)>,
   118→}
   119→
   120→/// Runtime context about connected remote nodes.
   121→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
   122→pub struct PromptNodesRuntimeContext {
   123→    pub nodes: Vec<PromptNodeInfo>,
   124→    pub default_node_id: Option<String>,
   125→}
   126→
   127→/// Combined runtime context injected into the system prompt.
   128→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]

   130→    pub host: PromptHostRuntimeContext,
   131→    pub sandbox: Option<PromptSandboxRuntimeContext>,

   712→fn append_runtime_datetime_tail(
   713→    prompt: &mut String,
   714→    runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>,
   715→) {
   716→    let Some(runtime) = runtime_context else {
   717→        return;

   719→
   720→    if let Some(time) = runtime
   721→        .host

   733→        .host

   735→        .as_deref()
   736→        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())

   740→        prompt.push_str(".\n");
   741→    }

   743→
   744→fn push_non_empty_runtime_field(parts: &mut Vec<String>, key: &str, value: Option<&str>) {
   745→    if let Some(value) = value.filter(|value| !value.is_empty()) {
   746→        parts.push(format!("{key}={value}"));
   747→    }
   748→}
   749→
   750→fn format_host_runtime_line(host: &PromptHostRuntimeContext) -> Option<String> {
   751→    let mut parts = Vec::new();
   752→    for (key, value) in [
   753→        ("host", host.host.as_deref()),
   754→        ("os", host.os.as_deref()),
   755→        ("arch", host.arch.as_deref()),
   756→        ("shell", host.shell.as_deref()),
   757→        ("today", host.today.as_deref()),
   758→        ("provider", host.provider.as_deref()),
   759→        ("model", host.model.as_deref()),
   760→        ("session", host.session_key.as_deref()),
   761→        ("surface", host.surface.as_deref()),
   762→        ("session_kind", host.session_kind.as_deref()),
   763→        ("channel_type", host.channel_type.as_deref()),
   764→        ("channel_account", host.channel_account_id.as_deref()),
   765→        ("channel_chat_id", host.channel_chat_id.as_deref()),
   766→        ("channel_chat_type", host.channel_chat_type.as_deref()),
   767→        ("data_dir", host.data_dir.as_deref()),
   768→    ] {
   769→        push_non_empty_runtime_field(&mut parts, key, value);
   770→    }
   771→    if let Some(sudo_non_interactive) = host.sudo_non_interactive {
   772→        parts.push(format!("sudo_non_interactive={sudo_non_interactive}"));
   773→    }
   774→    for (key, value) in [
   775→        ("sudo_status", host.sudo_status.as_deref()),
   776→        ("timezone", host.timezone.as_deref()),
   777→        ("accept_language", host.accept_language.as_deref()),
   778→        ("remote_ip", host.remote_ip.as_deref()),
   779→        ("location", host.location.as_deref()),
   780→    ] {
   781→        push_non_empty_runtime_field(&mut parts, key, value);
   782→    }
   783→
   784→    (!parts.is_empty()).then(|| format!("Host: {}", parts.join(" | ")))
   785→}

   789→        return String::new();

   492→const NODE_ROUTING_GUIDANCE: &str = "\
   493→- When nodes are connected, the `exec` tool accepts an optional `node` parameter to target a specific node.\n\
   494→- Omitting `node` runs on the session's default node (shown as [default: ...] above), or locally if none is set.\n\
   495→- Use node telemetry (CPU, memory) to pick appropriate targets for resource-intensive tasks.\n\n";
   496→
   497→fn append_runtime_section(
   498→    prompt: &mut String,
   499→    runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>,
   500→    include_tools: bool,
   501→) {
   502→    let Some(runtime) = runtime_context else {
   503→        return;
   504→    };
   505→
   506→    let host_line = format_host_runtime_line(&runtime.host);
   507→    let sandbox_line = runtime.sandbox.as_ref().map(format_sandbox_runtime_line);
   508→    let nodes_line = runtime
   509→        .nodes
   510→        .as_ref()
   511→        .and_then(format_nodes_runtime_section);
   512→    if host_line.is_none() && sandbox_line.is_none() && nodes_line.is_none() {
   513→        return;
   514→    }
   515→
   516→    prompt.push_str("## Runtime\n\n");
   517→    if let Some(line) = host_line {
   518→        prompt.push_str(&line);
   519→        prompt.push('\n');
   520→    }
   521→    let has_sandbox = sandbox_line.is_some();
   522→    if let Some(line) = sandbox_line {
   523→        prompt.push_str(&line);
   524→        prompt.push('\n');
   525→    }
   526→    let has_nodes = nodes_line.is_some();
   527→    if let Some(line) = nodes_line {
   528→        prompt.push_str(&line);
   529→        prompt.push('\n');
   530→    }
   531→    if include_tools {
   532→        if has_sandbox {
   533→            prompt.push_str(EXEC_ROUTING_GUIDANCE_SANDBOX);
   534→        } else {
   535→            prompt.push_str(EXEC_ROUTING_GUIDANCE_HOST_ONLY);
   536→        }
   537→        if runtime.host.sudo_non_interactive == Some(true) {
   538→            prompt.push_str(EXEC_ROUTING_SUDO_HINT);
   539→        }
   540→        if has_sandbox {
   541→            prompt.push_str(EXEC_ROUTING_SANDBOX_CLOSING);
   542→        }
   543→        prompt.push('\n');
   544→        if has_nodes {
   545→            prompt.push_str(NODE_ROUTING_GUIDANCE);
   546→        }
   547→    } else {
   548→        prompt.push('\n');
   549→    }

  1165→async fn build_prompt_runtime_context(
  1166→    state: &Arc<dyn ChatRuntime>,
  1167→    provider: &Arc<dyn moltis_agents::model::LlmProvider>,
  1168→    session_key: &str,
  1169→    session_entry: Option<&SessionEntry>,
  1170→) -> PromptRuntimeContext {
  1171→    let data_dir = moltis_config::data_dir();
  1172→    let data_dir_display = data_dir.display().to_string();
  1173→
  1174→    let sudo_fut = detect_host_sudo_access();
  1175→    let sandbox_fut = async {
  1176→        if let Some(router) = state.sandbox_router() {
  1177→            let is_sandboxed = router.is_sandboxed(session_key).await;
  1178→            // Only include sandbox context when sandbox is actually enabled for
  1179→            // this session.  When disabled, omitting it prevents the LLM from
  1180→            // hallucinating sandbox usage (see #360).  This intentionally
  1181→            // discards `session_override` — its only consumer is the prompt
  1182→            // line we are omitting, and no other code reads it from
  1183→            // `PromptSandboxRuntimeContext`.
  1184→            if !is_sandboxed {
  1185→                return None;
  1186→            }
  1187→            let config = router.config();
  1188→            let backend_name = router.backend_name();
  1189→            let workspace_mount = config.workspace_mount.to_string();
  1190→            let workspace_path = (workspace_mount != "none").then(|| data_dir_display.clone());
  1191→            Some(PromptSandboxRuntimeContext {
  1192→                exec_sandboxed: true,
  1193→                mode: Some(config.mode.to_string()),
  1194→                backend: Some(backend_name.to_string()),
  1195→                scope: Some(config.scope.to_string()),
  1196→                image: Some(router.resolve_image(session_key, None).await),
  1197→                home: Some("/home/sandbox".to_string()),
  1198→                workspace_mount: Some(workspace_mount),
  1199→                workspace_path,
  1200→                no_network: prompt_sandbox_no_network_state(backend_name, config.no_network),
  1201→                session_override: session_entry.and_then(|entry| entry.sandbox_enabled),
  1202→            })
  1203→        } else {
  1204→            None
  1205→        }
  1206→    };
  1207→
  1208→    let ((sudo_non_interactive, sudo_status), sandbox_ctx) = tokio::join!(sudo_fut, sandbox_fut);
  1209→
  1210→    let configured_timezone = state
  1211→        .sandbox_router()
  1212→        .and_then(|r| r.config().timezone.clone());
  1213→    let timezone = Some(server_prompt_timezone(configured_timezone.as_deref()));
  1214→
  1215→    let location = state
  1216→        .cached_location()
  1217→        .await
  1218→        .as_ref()
  1219→        .map(|loc| loc.to_string());
  1220→    let channel_context = resolve_channel_runtime_context(session_key, session_entry);
  1221→
  1222→    let mut host_ctx = PromptHostRuntimeContext {
  1223→        host: Some(state.hostname().to_string()),
  1224→        os: Some(std::env::consts::OS.to_string()),
  1225→        arch: Some(std::env::consts::ARCH.to_string()),
  1226→        shell: detect_runtime_shell(),
  1227→        time: None,
  1228→        provider: Some(provider.name().to_string()),
  1229→        model: Some(provider.id().to_string()),
  1230→        session_key: Some(session_key.to_string()),
  1231→        surface: channel_context.surface,
  1232→        session_kind: channel_context.session_kind,

Now I have all the context needed. Here's the implementation plan:

---

## Implementation Plan

The plan has 4 phases with decreasing priority. Here's my recommended approach for each:

### Phase 1: Stabilize datetime for local providers (highest impact)

**Option A (recommended)** — Coarsen to date-only for local providers:

1. **`crates/agents/src/model.rs`** — Add `fn is_local(&self) -> bool` to `LlmProvider` trait with default `false`
2. **`crates/providers/src/openai.rs`** — Store `local_only: bool` in `OpenAiProvider`, override `is_local()` to return it. The `local_only` field already exists on `OpenAiCompatDef` — just thread it through to the provider instance.
3. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — Change `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` to accept a `local_provider: bool` param. When true, set `host.time = None` (only `host.today` remains). The `today` field changes once per day → KV cache valid all day.
4. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1244`** — Pass `provider.is_local()` when calling `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()`
5. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1385`** — Same for the `apply_request_runtime_context()` call path (needs the flag too)

**Also handle `local_gguf` provider** if it implements `LlmProvider` — it's inherently local.

### Phase 2: Stabilize node telemetry

**`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs:439-474`** — In `format_node_runtime_line()`, round volatile values:
- `cpu_usage` → nearest 10% (`(usage / 10.0).round() * 10.0`)
- `mem_available` → nearest GB (already formatted as `{:.0}GB` but the raw value changes)
- `disk_available` → same rounding

This reduces churn from ~per-second to only when values cross a 10%/1GB boundary.

### Phase 3: Move datetime to end of messages

This is more invasive and should be done separately:
1. Remove `append_runtime_datetime_tail()` from `build_system_prompt_full()`
2. In the agent runner or chat handler, inject a short `ChatMessage::System` with just the datetime at the end of the message list (before the latest user message)
3. Validate that mid-conversation system messages work correctly with Ollama/Qwen

### Phase 4: Ollama native API — future work, park it.

---

**Want me to implement Phase 1 now?** That's the biggest win — eliminating per-second datetime changes for Ollama/LM Studio providers. I can also bundle Phase 2 (node telemetry rounding) since it's small and independent.

> DEVELOPER

Would adding a tool for date/datetime or adding those at the end of the prompt help? Same things about the node telemetry adding a tool for it instead?

> AGENT

Good questions. Let me think through both ideas.

## DateTime as a tool vs. end-of-prompt

**Tool for date/time:**
- Local LLMs would need to *decide* to call a tool just to know the current time, adding a round-trip on every turn where time matters
- Tool schemas are already large and stable — adding another one is fine for cache, but it makes the LLM work harder for basic contextual info
- Most LLMs expect datetime in the system prompt as grounding context; a tool makes it opt-in rather than always-available
- Cloud providers (Anthropic, OpenAI) benefit from datetime in the prompt for prompt caching too — it's a well-established pattern

**Datetime at end of messages (Phase 3 in the plan):**
- This is the sweet spot. The system prompt stays byte-identical between turns (perfect KV cache hit for the entire prefix). Only the last few tokens (a short system message with the datetime) need re-processing
- Works with both local and cloud providers
- The LLM still *sees* the datetime every turn without needing to call anything
- Risk: some models handle mid-conversation system messages poorly, but most OpenAI-compatible models (including Ollama-served ones) handle them fine

**Verdict:** End-of-messages is strictly better than a tool. It preserves the "always available" property while not busting the cache.

## Node telemetry as a tool

This is actually a **much better fit** for a tool than datetime is:

- The LLM rarely needs real-time CPU/memory stats — it only matters when making routing decisions for `exec` commands
- A `node_status` tool would let the LLM query telemetry on-demand, keeping the system prompt completely stable
- The current approach stuffs volatile data into every request even when the LLM doesn't care
- Removes the prompt from being coupled to telemetry polling frequency

**Downside:** the LLM currently uses node telemetry in the system prompt to pick which node to target for `exec`. With a tool, it would need to first call `node_status`, then decide. That's one extra round-trip, but only when nodes are actually used.

## Recommended approach

1. **Phase 1:** Move datetime to end of messages (your Phase 3 idea, skip the coarsen-for-local hack — this is cleaner and benefits all providers)
2. **Phase 2:** Replace inline node telemetry with a `node_status` tool — keep only static node info (name, platform, capabilities) in the system prompt, move volatile stats to the tool

This eliminates *both* sources of cache invalidation without provider-specific branching. Want me to implement this approach instead?

## Summary

I've thoroughly analyzed the tool registration system and found the key components for creating a `node_status` tool. Here's what I found:

### 1. **AgentTool Trait Definition**
Location: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/agents/src/tool_registry.rs` (lines 10-21)

```rust
#[async_trait]
pub trait AgentTool: Send + Sync {
    fn name(&self) -> &str;
    fn description(&self) -> &str;
    fn parameters_schema(&self) -> serde_json::Value;
    async fn warmup(&self) -> Result<()> { Ok(()) }
    async fn execute(&self, params: serde_json::Value) -> Result<serde_json::Value>;
}
```

The trait requires:
- `name()` - Returns tool name string (e.g., "node_status")
- `description()` - Human-readable description for the LLM
- `parameters_schema()` - JSON Schema object for parameters
- `warmup()` - Optional initialization (optional, defaults to Ok())
- `execute()` - Async function that performs the actual tool logic

### 2. **Simple Tool Example: CalcTool**
Location: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/tools/src/calc.rs`

Complete implementation pattern:
```rust
use {
    async_trait::async_trait,
    moltis_agents::tool_registry::AgentTool,
    serde_json::{Value, json},
};

#[derive(Default)]
pub struct CalcTool;

impl CalcTool {
    #[must_use]
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self
    }
}

#[async_trait]
impl AgentTool for CalcTool {
    fn name(&self) -> &str {
        "calc"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &str {
        "Evaluate arithmetic expressions safely..."
    }

    fn parameters_schema(&self) -> Value {
        json!({
            "type": "object",
            "required": ["expression"],
            "properties": {
                "expression": {
                    "type": "string",
                    "description": "Arithmetic expression to evaluate"
                },
                "expr": {
                    "type": "string",
                    "description": "Alias for expression"
                }
            }
        })
    }

    async fn execute(&self, params: Value) -> anyhow::Result<Value> {
        let expression = params
            .get("expression")
            .or_else(|| params.get("expr"))
            .and_then(Value::as_str)
            .ok_or_else(|| Error::message("missing 'expression' parameter"))?;

        let (result, normalized_expr) = evaluate_expression(expression)?;
        Ok(json!({
            "result": result_to_json(result)?,
            "normalized_expr": normalized_expr
        }))
    }
}
```

### 3. **Provider-Based Tool Example: LocationTool**
Location: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/tools/src/location.rs` (lines 258-402)

Pattern for tools that need external provider:
```rust
pub struct LocationTool {
    requester: Arc<dyn LocationRequester>,
}

impl LocationTool {
    pub fn new(requester: Arc<dyn LocationRequester>) -> Self {
        Self { requester }
    }
}

#[async_trait]
impl AgentTool for LocationTool {
    fn name(&self) -> &str {
        "get_user_location"
    }
    // ... rest of trait methods
    
    async fn execute(&self, params: serde_json::Value) -> anyhow::Result<serde_json::Value> {
        // Implementation that uses self.requester
    }
}
```

### 4. **Tool Registration in Gateway**
Location: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/gateway/src/server.rs` (line 3611 onwards)

Tools are registered in the `setup_gateway()` function:
```rust
let mut tool_registry = moltis_agents::tool_registry::ToolRegistry::new();

// Simple stateless tools
tool_registry.register(Box::new(moltis_tools::calc::CalcTool::new()));

// Tools with dependencies
tool_registry.register(Box::new(moltis_tools::location::LocationTool::new(
    Arc::new(some_provider),
)));

// Node tools specifically (lines 3713-3727):
let node_info_provider: Arc<dyn moltis_tools::nodes::NodeInfoProvider> = Arc::new(
    crate::node_exec::GatewayNodeInfoProvider::new(Arc::clone(&state)),
);
tool_registry.register(Box::new(moltis_tools::nodes::NodesListTool::new(
    Arc::clone(&node_info_provider),
)));
```

### 5. **Node Telemetry Data Structure**
Location: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/tools/src/nodes.rs` (lines 18-46)

The `NodeInfo` struct contains:
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct NodeInfo {
    pub node_id: String,
    pub display_name: Option<String>,
    pub platform: String,
    pub capabilities: Vec<String>,
    pub commands: Vec<String>,
    pub remote_ip: Option<String>,
    // ── Telemetry ──
    pub mem_total: Option<u64>,
    pub mem_available: Option<u64>,
    pub cpu_count: Option<u32>,
    pub cpu_usage: Option<f32>,
    pub uptime_secs: Option<u64>,
    pub services: Vec<String>,
    pub telemetry_stale: bool,
    pub disk_total: Option<u64>,
    pub disk_available: Option<u64>,
    pub runtimes: Vec<String>,
    pub providers: Vec<NodeProviderInfo>,
}
```

### 6. **How Node Telemetry is Populated**
Location chain:
1. **Data source**: `crates/gateway/src/nodes.rs` - `NodeSession` struct holds raw telemetry data
2. **Conversion**: `crates/gateway/src/node_exec.rs` (lines 360-389) - `node_to_info()` converts `NodeSession` to `NodeInfo`
3. **Provider**: `crates/gateway/src/node_exec.rs` (lines 391-447) - `GatewayNodeInfoProvider` implements `NodeInfoProvider` trait
4. **Tool access**: `crates/tools/src/nodes.rs` (lines 88-120) - `NodesListTool` and `NodesDescribeTool` call the provider
5. **Prompt injection**: `crates/chat/src/lib.rs` (lines 1255-1270) - Converts to `PromptNodeInfo` for system prompt

The telemetry fields (`cpu_usage`, `mem_available`, etc.) are updated by `node.telemetry` events coming from connected remote nodes, stored in `NodeSession`, and marked as stale if last_telemetry timestamp is >120 seconds old.

### 7. **Tool Registration Methods**
`ToolRegistry` supports three registration methods:
```rust
pub fn register(&mut self, tool: Box<dyn AgentTool>)  // Built-in
pub fn register_mcp(&mut self, tool: Box<dyn AgentTool>, server: String)  // MCP
pub fn register_wasm(&mut self, tool: Box<dyn AgentTool>, component_hash: [u8; 32])  // WASM
```

All files are in these locations:
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/agents/src/tool_registry.rs` - AgentTool trait
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/tools/src/calc.rs` - Simple tool example
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/tools/src/nodes.rs` - Node data types
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/gateway/src/node_exec.rs` - Node provider impl
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/gateway/src/server.rs` - Registration location
- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/chat/src/lib.rs` - System prompt injection

Let me now implement both changes. Here's the plan:

**Change 1: Move datetime to end of messages**
- Remove `append_runtime_datetime_tail()` call from `build_system_prompt_full()` 
- Add a new public function to build a datetime system message
- In `run_agent_loop_streaming()`, inject the datetime message at the end of messages (after history, before user message)
- Update `apply_voice_reply_suffix` to no longer need to preserve datetime tail positioning
- Update tests

**Change 2: Remove volatile node telemetry from system prompt**
- In `format_node_runtime_line()`, remove `cpu_usage`, `mem_available`, `disk_available` 
- Update `PromptNodeInfo` to remove volatile fields (they exist in the tools already)
- Update the node guidance text to mention using `nodes_list` tool for telemetry
- Update the node context construction in chat handler

  "new_string": "/// Build a short datetime string suitable for injection as a trailing system\n/// message, keeping the main system prompt stable for KV cache locality.\n///\n/// Returns `None` when the runtime context has neither `time` nor `today`.\n#[must_use]\npub fn runtime_datetime_message(runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>) -> Option<String> {\n    let runtime = runtime_context?;\n\n    if let Some(time) = runtime\n        .host\n        .time\n        .as_deref()\n        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())\n    {\n        return Some(format!(\"The current user datetime is {time}.\"));\n    }\n\n    runtime\n        .host\n        .today\n        .as_deref()\n        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())\n        .map(|today| format!(\"The current user date is {today}.\"))\n}\n\n/// Maximum number of characters from `MEMORY.md` injected into the system\n/// prompt to keep the context window manageable."
}

It's called from many places. Adding a new parameter would be invasive. A better approach: **inject the datetime message in the chat handler** by appending it to the history before passing to `run_agent_loop_streaming`. This way the runner doesn't need to change.

Actually, let me reconsider. Looking at the runner code at line 1256:

```rust
let mut messages: Vec<ChatMessage> = vec![ChatMessage::system(system_prompt)];
if let Some(hist) = history { messages.extend(hist); }
messages.push(ChatMessage::User { content: user_content.clone() });
```

The order is: `[system_prompt, ...history, user_message]`. I want datetime injected as: `[system_prompt, ...history, datetime_system_msg, user_message]`.

The cleanest approach: in the chat handler (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`), append the datetime system message to the history before calling `run_agent_loop_streaming`. Let me do that.

  "replace_all": false,

}

Now strip the volatile fields (`cpu_usage`, `mem_available`, `disk_available`) from the node runtime line in the system prompt. The `nodes_list` tool already provides this data on-demand.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/shiny-anise/crates/agents/src/prompt.rs",
  "old_string": "fn format_node_runtime_line(node: &PromptNodeInfo) -> String {\n    let name = node.display_name.as_deref().unwrap_or(&node.node_id);\n    let mut parts = vec![node.platform.clone()];\n    if !node.capabilities.is_empty() {\n        parts.push(format!(\"caps: {}\", node.capabilities.join(\",\")));\n    }\n    if let Some(cpus) = node.cpu_count {\n        parts.push(format!(\"{cpus} cores\"));\n    }\n    if let Some(usage) = node.cpu_usage {\n        parts.push(format!(\"{usage:.0}% cpu\"));\n    }\n    if let (Some(avail), Some(total)) = (node.mem_available, node.mem_total) {\n        let avail_gb = avail as f64 / 1_073_741_824.0;\n        let total_gb = total as f64 / 1_073_741_824.0;\n        parts.push(format!(\"{avail_gb:.0}GB/{total_gb:.0}GB mem\"));\n    }\n    if let (Some(avail), Some(total)) = (node.disk_available, node.disk_total) {\n        let avail_gb = avail as f64 / 1_073_741_824.0;\n        let total_gb = total as f64 / 1_073_741_824.0;\n        parts.push(format!(\"disk: {avail_gb:.0}GB/{total_gb:.0}GB free\"));\n    }\n    if !node.runtimes.is_empty() {\n        parts.push(format!(\"runtimes: {}\", node.runtimes.join(\",\")));\n    }\n    if !node.providers.is_empty() {\n        let names: Vec<&str> = node.providers.iter().map(|(n, _)| n.as_str()).collect();\n        parts.push(format!(\"providers: {}\", names.join(\",\")));\n    }\n    let suffix = if node.telemetry_stale {\n        \" (stale)\"\n    } else {\n        \"\"\n    };\n    format!(\"{name} ({}{suffix})\", parts.join(\", \"))\n}",
  "new_string": "fn format_node_runtime_line(node: &PromptNodeInfo) -> String {\n    let name = node.display_name.as_deref().unwrap_or(&node.node_id);\n    let mut parts = vec![node.platform.clone()];\n    if !node.capabilities.is_empty() {\n        parts.push(format!(\"caps: {}\", node.capabilities.join(\",\")));\n    }\n    if let Some(cpus) = node.cpu_count {\n        parts.push(format!(\"{cpus} cores\"));\n    }\n    // Volatile telemetry (cpu_usage, mem_available, disk_available) is omitted\n    // from the system prompt to keep it stable for KV cache locality.\n    // Use the `nodes_list` or `nodes_describe` tools for live telemetry.\n    if let Some(total) = node.mem_total {\n        let total_gb = total as f64 / 1_073_741_824.0;\n        parts.push(format!(\"{total_gb:.0}GB mem\"));\n    }\n    if !node.runtimes.is_empty() {\n        parts.push(format!(\"runtimes: {}\", node.runtimes.join(\",\")));\n    }\n    if !node.providers.is_empty() {\n        let names: Vec<&str> = node.providers.iter().map(|(n, _)| n.as_str()).collect();\n        parts.push(format!(\"providers: {}\", names.join(\",\")));\n    }\n    format!(\"{name} ({})\", parts.join(\", \"))\n}"
}

  "new_string": "/// Info about a single connected remote node, injected into the system prompt.\n///\n/// Only stable fields are included here; volatile telemetry (cpu_usage,\n/// mem_available, disk_available) is served on-demand via the `nodes_list`\n/// / `nodes_describe` tools to avoid invalidating the KV cache.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct PromptNodeInfo {\n    pub node_id: String,\n    pub display_name: Option<String>,\n    pub platform: String,\n    pub capabilities: Vec<String>,\n    pub cpu_count: Option<u32>,\n    pub mem_total: Option<u64>,\n    pub runtimes: Vec<String>,\n    /// `(provider_name, model_list)` pairs discovered on the node.\n    pub providers: Vec<(String, Vec<String>)>,\n}"
}

the system prompt — it is injected as a\n        // trailing system message for KV cache stability.\n        assert!(!prompt.contains(\"The current user datetime is\"));\n        assert!(!prompt.contains(\"The current user date is\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_runtime_datetime_message_returns_time_when_present() {\n        let runtime = PromptRuntimeContext {\n            host: PromptHostRuntimeContext {\n                time: Some(\"2026-02-17 16:18:00 CET\".into()),\n                today: Some(\"2026-02-17\".into()),\n                ..Default::default()\n            },\n            sandbox: None,\n            nodes: None,\n        };\n\n        let msg = runtime_datetime_message(Some(&runtime));\n        assert_eq!(\n            msg.as_deref(),\n            Some(\"The current user datetime is 2026-02-17 16:18:00 CET.\")\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_runtime_datetime_message_falls_back_to_today() {\n        let runtime = PromptRuntimeContext {\n            host: PromptHostRuntimeContext {\n                today: Some(\"2026-02-17\".into()),\n                ..Default::default()\n            },\n            sandbox: None,\n            nodes: None,\n        };\n\n        let msg = runtime_datetime_message(Some(&runtime));\n        assert_eq!(\n            msg.as_deref(),\n            Some(\"The current user date is 2026-02-17.\")\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_runtime_datetime_message_returns_none_without_time_or_date() {\n        let runtime = PromptRuntimeContext {\n            host: PromptHostRuntimeContext::default(),\n            sandbox: None,\n            nodes: None,\n        };\n\n        assert!(runtime_datetime_message(Some(&runtime)).is_none());\n        assert!(runtime_datetime_message(None).is_none());\n    }"

> AGENT

All tests pass — 0 failures across the entire workspace. Here's a summary of what was implemented:

## Changes Made

### 1. Move datetime from system prompt to end-of-messages injection

**`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`:**
- Removed `append_runtime_datetime_tail()` call from `build_system_prompt_full()` — the system prompt no longer contains per-second datetime strings
- Removed the now-unused `append_runtime_datetime_tail()` function
- Added new public `runtime_datetime_message()` function that returns the datetime string for injection as a separate message
- Updated tests: replaced 3 old datetime-tail tests with 4 new tests validating the new approach

**`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`:**
- In both chat handler paths (streaming + non-streaming), inject `ChatMessage::system(datetime)` **after** conversation history, so it appears just before the user message
- Simplified `apply_voice_reply_suffix()` — no longer needs the `runtime_context` parameter or the datetime-tail repositioning logic
- Removed `runtime_datetime_prompt_tail()` helper (dead code)
- Updated voice suffix tests

**Effect:** The system prompt is now byte-identical between turns within a session. Only the trailing datetime system message (a few tokens) changes, so the KV cache prefix match covers the entire system prompt + conversation history.

### 2. Remove volatile node telemetry from system prompt

**`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`:**
- Removed `cpu_usage`, `mem_available`, `disk_available`, `telemetry_stale` from `PromptNodeInfo` struct
- Updated `format_node_runtime_line()` to only include stable fields (platform, capabilities, cpu_count, mem_total, runtimes, providers)
- Updated node routing guidance to point users to `nodes_list`/`nodes_describe` tools for live telemetry

**`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`:**
- Updated `PromptNodeInfo` construction to match the reduced struct

**Effect:** The Nodes section of the system prompt no longer changes when CPU/memory/disk usage fluctuates. The existing `nodes_list` and `nodes_describe` tools already serve this data on-demand.

     1→# Stabilize System Prompt for Local LLM KV Cache
     2→
     3→## Problem
     4→
     5→Local LLM backends (Ollama, LM Studio) use KV cache to skip re-processing
     6→tokens that are identical to the previous request's prefix. Moltis invalidates
     7→this cache on **every turn** because the system prompt contains a per-second
     8→datetime string that changes each request. This forces 15-20s of full prompt
     9→re-ingestion on every message, even though the actual conversation content is
    10→mostly unchanged.
    11→
    12→When the same question is asked directly in LM Studio (no system prompt, no
    13→tools), the response is instant because the KV cache prefix matches.
    14→
    15→## Root Causes
    16→
    17→### 1. Per-second datetime in system prompt (primary)
    18→
    19→`crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1282` — `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` is called per
    20→turn, setting `host.time` to the current second. This flows into two places in
    21→the rendered system prompt:
    22→
    23→- **Runtime section** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs:497`): `Host: ... time=2026-03-23 14:32:17 CET`
    24→- **Tail line** (`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs:726`): `The current user datetime is 2026-03-23 14:32:17 CET.`
    25→
    26→Since the system prompt is the **first** content in the rendered prompt, any
    27→change invalidates the KV cache for **all** subsequent tokens (entire
    28→conversation history + tool schemas).
    29→
    30→### 2. Dynamic node telemetry in Runtime section
    31→
    32→When remote nodes are connected, `cpu_usage`, `mem_available`, `disk_available`
    33→are included in the `## Runtime` section. These change frequently and also
    34→invalidate the cache.
    35→
    36→### 3. Tool schemas are large but stable (amplifier, not cause)
    37→
    38→Tool schemas add ~1000-3000 tokens per request. They don't change between
    39→turns, but they must be fully re-ingested whenever the system prompt prefix
    40→changes. This amplifies the cost of cause #1.
    41→
    42→## Plan
    43→
    44→### Phase 1: Stabilize datetime within a session (quick win, biggest impact)
    45→
    46→**Goal**: Make the system prompt byte-identical between turns within the same
    47→session so the KV cache prefix matches.
    48→
    49→#### Option A: Coarsen datetime to date-only for local providers (recommended)
    50→
    51→In `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()` (`crates/chat/src/lib.rs:1282`), when the
    52→provider is a local backend (Ollama, LM Studio, or any provider with a
    53→localhost/private base URL), set `host.time = None` and only set `host.today`
    54→(date without time). The date changes once per day, so the KV cache is valid
    55→for an entire day's session.
    56→
    57→**Files to change:**
    58→
    59→1. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — Add a `is_local_provider` check (or pass a
    60→   flag) to `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()`. When local, skip setting
    61→   `host.time` and only set `host.today`.
    62→
    63→2. **`crates/providers/src/lib.rs`** — Expose a way to determine if a provider
    64→   is local. The Ollama registration path already knows this (`def.config_name
    65→   == "ollama"`). Consider adding a `is_local: bool` field to `ModelInfo` or a
    66→   method on the provider trait.
    67→
    68→3. **`crates/agents/src/model.rs`** — Add `fn is_local(&self) -> bool` to
    69→   `LlmProvider` trait with a default `false` implementation. Override in
    70→   `OpenAiProvider` when the base URL is localhost/private.
    71→
    72→**Alternatively** (simpler, less targeted): Cache the runtime context per
    73→session and only regenerate it every N minutes or when config changes. This
    74→avoids the local-vs-remote distinction but means cloud providers also get a
    75→slightly stale datetime.
    76→
    77→#### Option B: Pin datetime at session start
    78→
    79→Store the datetime when the session starts and reuse it for all subsequent
    80→turns. This is the simplest approach but means the LLM sees a stale time if the
    81→conversation runs for hours.
    82→
    83→**Files to change:**
    84→
    85→1. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — In `build_prompt_runtime_context()`, accept
    86→   an optional `pinned_time: Option<String>`. If provided, use it instead of
    87→   calling `refresh_runtime_prompt_time()`.
    88→
    89→2. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — In the main chat handler (around line 3457),
    90→   store the first runtime context's time value and pass it to subsequent
    91→   calls.
    92→
    93→### Phase 2: Stabilize node telemetry (if nodes are used)
    94→
    95→When remote nodes are connected, their telemetry (`cpu_usage`, `mem_available`)
    96→changes between turns. Two options:
    97→
    98→1. **Exclude volatile telemetry from the system prompt** — Move `cpu_usage`,
    99→   `mem_available`, `disk_available` out of the Runtime section. The LLM rarely
   100→   needs real-time resource stats in the system prompt.
   101→
   102→2. **Round/bucket telemetry values** — Round CPU to nearest 10%, memory to
   103→   nearest GB. This reduces churn while keeping the info available.
   104→
   105→**Files to change:**
   106→
   107→- `crates/agents/src/prompt.rs` — `format_nodes_runtime_section()` (around line
   108→  508). Either skip volatile fields or round them.
   109→
   110→### Phase 3: Move datetime to end of messages (structural improvement)
   111→
   112→Even with Phase 1, the datetime is inside the system prompt which is the first
   113→message. An alternative architecture: remove datetime from the system prompt
   114→entirely and inject it as a short system message **at the end** of the message
   115→list, just before the latest user message. This way, even if the datetime
   116→changes, only the last few tokens need re-processing (the KV cache for the
   117→entire prior conversation is preserved).
   118→
   119→**Files to change:**
   120→
   121→1. **`crates/agents/src/prompt.rs`** — Remove `append_runtime_datetime_tail()`
   122→   from `build_system_prompt_full()`.
   123→
   124→2. **`crates/chat/src/lib.rs`** — In `run_with_tools()` (around line 6040),
   125→   remove `apply_voice_reply_suffix` datetime handling. Instead, after building
   126→   the message history, append a `ChatMessage::System` with the datetime just
   127→   before sending to the provider.
   128→
   129→3. **`crates/providers/src/openai.rs`** — In `serialize_messages_for_request()`,
   130→   ensure trailing system messages are preserved (they currently are, since
   131→   system messages just map to `{"role": "system", ...}`).
   132→
   133→**Trade-off**: Some providers/models handle mid-conversation system messages
   134→poorly. Test with Ollama + Qwen specifically. This approach gives the best KV
   135→cache behavior but needs validation.
   136→
   137→### Phase 4: Explore Ollama-native API (future)
   138→
   139→Moltis currently uses Ollama's OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions`
   140→endpoint. Ollama's native `/api/chat` endpoint offers:
   141→
   142→- Explicit `keep_alive` parameter to control model unload timeout
   143→- Same KV caching behavior but with more control
   144→- Native format avoids OpenAI compatibility layer overhead
   145→
   146→This is a larger change and not required if Phases 1-3 resolve the issue. Park
   147→it unless users report additional problems.
   148→
   149→## Recommended Implementation Order
   150→
   151→1. **Phase 1 Option A** — Biggest bang for buck. Stabilize datetime for local
   152→   providers. This alone should eliminate the 15-20s re-ingestion.
   153→2. **Phase 2** — Only if users have remote nodes connected and still see cache
   154→   misses.
   155→3. **Phase 3** — Best long-term architecture but more invasive. Do this if
   156→   Phase 1 isn't sufficient or if you want to optimize for cloud providers too
   157→   (Anthropic prompt caching also benefits from stable prefixes).
   158→4. **Phase 4** — Future work, only if needed.
   159→
   160→## Validation
   161→
   162→- Set up Ollama with Qwen 3.4 locally
   163→- Send 3-4 messages in a conversation
   164→- Before fix: observe prompt eval time in Ollama logs (`prompt eval time: ...`)
   165→  growing with each turn and re-processing all tokens
   166→- After fix: observe prompt eval time staying small (only new tokens processed)
   167→- Ollama debug logs: `OLLAMA_DEBUG=1 ollama serve` shows cache hit/miss info
   168→- Also verify cloud providers (OpenAI, Anthropic) still get correct datetime
   169→
```

**Summary of what's done vs. remaining:**

**Done:**
- Phase 2 (node telemetry) — volatile fields removed from prompt
- Phase 3 (move datetime to end of messages) — datetime removed from system prompt, injected as trailing system message
- Phase 3 item 3 verification — `serialize_messages_for_request()` preserves trailing system messages correctly

**Remaining (minor):**
- The `Host:` line still has `today=YYYY-MM-DD` which changes once per day. This is a ~1 cache bust per day, not per second, so it's low priority. Removing it would mean the LLM has no date context in the system prompt at all (the trailing message covers it).
- **Manual validation** with Ollama + Qwen per the plan's Validation section — needs a running Ollama instance to verify `prompt eval time` stays small across turns.
- **Phase 4** (Ollama native API) — explicitly parked as future work.

Now the system prompt structure is:

- **Stable prefix** (identity, project context, runtime Host line without `today`, tools, guidelines) — byte-identical between turns
- **Tail** — `"Today is 2026-03-24."` — changes once per day
- **Trailing system message** (separate from prompt) — `"The current user datetime is 2026-03-24 01:23:45 CET."` — changes per turn, but only costs re-processing a few tokens since it's at the end of the message list

+/// Build a short datetime string suitable for injection as a trailing system
+/// message, keeping the main system prompt stable for KV cache locality.
+///
+/// Returns `None` when the runtime context has neither `time` nor `today`.
+#[must_use]
+pub fn runtime_datetime_message(runtime_context: Option<&PromptRuntimeContext>) -> Option<String> {
+    let runtime = runtime_context?;
+
+    if let Some(time) = runtime
+        .host
+        .time
+        .as_deref()
+        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())
+    {
+        return Some(format!("The current user datetime is {time}."));
+    }
+
+    runtime
+        .host
+        .today
+        .as_deref()
+        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())
+        .map(|today| format!("The current user date is {today}."))
+}

This keeps the system prompt\nbyte-identical between turns, enabling KV cache prefix hits for local\nLLMs (Ollama, LM Studio) and prompt-cache hits for cloud providers.\n\nRemove volatile node telemetry (cpu_usage, mem_available,\ndisk_available) from the system prompt Runtime section. These stats\nchange frequently and invalidate the cache. }

   127→    for msg in messages {
   128→        match msg {
   129→            ChatMessage::System { content } => {

   131→                    Some(existing) => format!("{existing}\n\n{content}"),
   132→                    None => content.clone(),
   133→                });
   134→            },
   135→            ChatMessage::User { content } => match content {
   136→                UserContent::Text(text) => {
   137→                    out.push(serde_json::json!({"role": "user", "content": text}));

   139→                UserContent::Multimodal(parts) => {
   140→                    let blocks: Vec<serde_json::Value> = parts

   143→                            ContentPart::Text(text) => {

   152→                                        "data": data,
   153→                                    }
   154→                                })
   155→                            },
   156→                        })

   158→                    out.push(serde_json::json!({"role": "user", "content": blocks}));

   161→            ChatMessage::Assistant {
   162→                content,
   163→                tool_calls,
   164→            } => {
   165→                if tool_calls.is_empty() {
   166→                    out.push(serde_json::json!({
   167→                        "role": "assistant",
   168→                        "content": content.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),
   169→                    }));
   170→                } else {
   171→                    let mut blocks = Vec::new();
   172→                    if let Some(text) = content
   173→                        && !text.is_empty()
   174→                    {
   175→                        blocks.push(serde_json::json!({"type": "text", "text": text}));
   176→                    }
   177→                    for tc in tool_calls {
   178→                        blocks.push(serde_json::json!({
   179→                            "type": "tool_use",
   180→                            "id": tc.id,
   181→                            "name": tc.name,
   182→                            "input": tc.arguments,
   183→                        }));
   184→                    }
   185→                    out.push(serde_json::json!({"role": "assistant", "content": blocks}));
   186→                }
   187→            },
   188→            ChatMessage::Tool {
   189→                tool_call_id,
   190→                content,
   191→            } => {
   192→                out.push(serde_json::json!({
   193→                    "role": "user",
   194→                    "content": [{
   195→                        "type": "tool_result",
   196→                        "tool_use_id": tool_call_id,
   197→                        "content": content,
   198→                    }]
   199→                }));
   200→            },
   201→        }
   202→    }

> AGENT

The fix: instead of merging all system messages into the `system` field, keep the **first** system message (the main prompt) as the stable `system` field, and convert any subsequent system messages into user-role text blocks. This preserves Anthropic prompt caching for the main prompt while still delivering the datetime.

> AGENT

Anthropic's API requires alternating user/assistant messages. Two consecutive user messages will error. I need a different approach. Let me look at how the messages flow — the trailing system message is injected after history but before the user message. In the Anthropic conversion, it'll become `user` (datetime) followed by `user` (actual message).

Better approach: merge the trailing system content into the next user message as a prefix, or use Anthropic's content blocks with `cache_control`. Actually, the simplest correct fix: for Anthropic, keep the current behavior of merging into the system field. The primary goal is local LLM KV cache, and Anthropic's prompt caching is a separate optimization.

Let me reconsider. The cleanest approach: keep all system messages in the `system` field for Anthropic (current behavior before my change), but use `cache_control` breakpoints to mark where the stable prefix ends. However, that's a bigger change. For now, the simplest correct fix is to merge trailing system messages as a content block within the **preceding** user message, or just revert the Anthropic change and note it in the PR.

Actually, looking more carefully: in the actual message flow, the datetime system message is inserted **before** the user message. So in the `out` array, we'd get `[...history, user("datetime"), user("actual question")]` — that's the consecutive user problem.

The safest fix: for Anthropic, keep merging all system messages into the top-level `system` field. The KV cache benefit for Anthropic requires `cache_control` annotations which is a separate feature. The PR's primary goal is local LLM cache stability, which works correctly for OpenAI-compat providers.

> AGENT

Done. The review comment is addressed and resolved:

- **Anthropic**: kept the existing merge-all-system-messages behavior since Anthropic requires alternating user/assistant messages. Added a comment explaining the constraint and a test. Prompt-cache stability for Anthropic via `cache_control` breakpoints is noted as future work.
- **Local LLMs & OpenAI-compat**: the optimization works as intended — trailing system messages stay in-place in the message list, keeping the system prompt prefix stable.

> DEVELOPER

Did you fix all comments from https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/476 including this one:


crates/chat/src/lib.rs, line 6618-6623 (link)

P1 Datetime missing from context-overflow retry

When ContextWindowExceeded triggers inline compaction, the compacted history is rebuilt from the store but the datetime system message is not re-injected. On the retry call the model receives no current date/time context — regressing the very thing this PR aims to fix.

The initial chat_history path at line 6534 does append the datetime, but the retry path at line 6618 only calls values_to_chat_messages and skips the injection entirely.
